Cross-episode issues

Ranked by impact on score validity, then frequency.

  1. In-flight go-to of a released takeover block is not cancelled. A-scheduling, high. 6 occurrences in 5 episodes.
  • Where:
  • ep 3 (master_hard_3) t3→t12 "go to the cheese source" → assistant false_alarm t15.
  • ep 4 (medium_hard_2) t119→t129 "go to the cheese source" delayed the meat rescue.
  • ep 5 (hard_hard_4) t106→t114 "go to the plates" delayed rescue of a smoking potato.
  • ep 8 (expert_hard_2) t295 "go to the potato source"; t336 "go to the egg source" after the egg mistake was prevented.
  • ep 9 (hard_medium_3) t120→t124 "go to the potato source" → assistant false_alarm t127.
  • Diagnosis: on release the harness drops queued commands, but the multi-tick walk that is already executing completes. ACTION ticks are completion ticks, and every gap (4-12 ticks) matches a walk begun before release. ep 9 t155→t158 ("go to board 1" completing after the cook replaced its plan) shows the same holds for any plan replace.
  • Fix: abort the current action at release (and on a cook "replace"), and resume from the cook's actual position.
  1. Attention-error commit and prevention timing contradicts the documented rule. F-grading, high, 5 cases in 4 episodes.
  • leave_unattended:
  • ep 2 t156: accepted before smoke and before block end, yet convinced_early_missed ... prevented=False.
  • ep 4 t119: accepted before COOKED, prevented=True.
  • forget_next_step:
  • ep 2 t264 and ep 9 t381: accepted while the block's final "wander/idle" was still pending ("[superseded]"), graded valid with prevented=False.
  • ep 6 t332: graded late while the cook was still frozen (release t335), 59 ticks after injection.
  • Diagnosis: the implementation appears to commit leave_unattended when the component reaches COOKED during the block, and forget_next_step soon after injection. Brief rule (d) says "block ran to its end or the heat smoked". The valid window (~30 ticks) is also shorter than the 62-tick forget_next_step max hold (eps 3, 6), so late verdicts land while the mistake is still live.
  • Fix: decide which is authoritative, then align the implementation and documentation. Cap the hold at the valid window, or extend the window to release.
  1. The cook announces the injected mistake aloud before or while it happens (design question: confirm intent). C/B plus validity, high.
  • Announcements:
  • ep 3 t0 "grab some cheese and get it chopped".
  • ep 4 t114 "next I'm going over to the cheese crate".
  • ep 8 t0 "grab an onion"; t330 "head down to the egg crate ... to add an egg to the plate".
  • ep 9 t116 "head back over to the potato crate"; t145 "heading over to the fish crate".
  • ep 10 t56 "I'm right at the tomato crate ... scooping some more into my plate".
  • search narrations: ep 1 t101, ep 8 t461.
  • Effect: 5 of the 6 early_correct verdicts in this sample (ep 3 t3, ep 4 t119, ep 8 t4, ep 8 t335, ep 9 t120) came within 5 ticks of such an announcement. The only exception is ep 2 t156. Preventions therefore largely measure listening to the cook, not watching the kitchen.
  • Diagnosis: the cook's takeover turn prompt invites it to verbalise its (injected) intent.
  • Fix: if not intended, have the takeover turn default to an empty utterance, consistent with "human acts mostly in silence".
  1. Flags credited on name alone, with false stated evidence. F/D, high, 4 cases.
  • Cases:
  • ep 4 t119 "The pan is smoking!", 5 ticks after turn-on, credited prevented=True.
  • ep 9 t120 "the fish in the middle fryer is smoking!", 5 ticks after turn-on, credited prevented=True.
  • ep 2 t156 fries "smoking" before they could be.
  • ep 5 t106 alias credited while the advice ignored the smoking fryer.
  • Diagnosis: the grader matches name, alias and timing only. The model over-predicts "smoking" on any lit station.
  • Fix: require the flag's component or station to match the injected one, or have the judge check the flag text.
  1. Post-release alias names graded false_alarm. F-grading, high, 2 cases.
  • Cases: ep 6 t71 (wrong_order after serve_raw put raw tomato first) and ep 6 t112 (wrong_order after misidentify put tomato before steak). The kitchen visibly supports both. In-window, the same alias is accepted (ep 1 t61 wrong_order→omit_subgoal, alias_in_window).
  • Diagnosis: the alias table is applied only inside the window, and "late" requires the exact name.
  • Fix: apply aliases in the late window, and grade post-release flags against the committed plate state before falling back to false_alarm.
  1. Answer capture: proactive requests never captured; answers mis-paired. F-grading.
  • Requests, medium, 7 of 7 show "answered tNone":
  • ep 2: fulfilled t75 "The steak is done cooking!"
  • ep 3: t120 reply.
  • ep 5: fulfilled t29.
  • ep 6: fulfilled t45.
  • ep 7.
  • ep 8: t97 reply.
  • ep 9: t209 "Will do."
  • Mis-paired, high, ep 7: q_past_done (t11) captured the answer to q_order (t32), and q_order captured an unrelated meat alert at t83.
  • Fix: close the capture window at the next voice ask, and record "no answer" rather than inheriting a later reply.
  1. Sustained assistant state hallucinations. D-assistant, high as a class (model-caused, correctly graded).
  • Loops that misled or would mislead a compliant cook:
  • ep 3 t273-t342: 11 replies, "cooked steak in the pan", 2 false_alarms.
  • ep 4 t104-t116: "pans cook automatically", "There is no turn on action".
  • ep 9 t198-t306: cooked fish in an empty pan, cooked potato in an unlit fryer; the novice complied and came within ~9 ticks of a fire.
  • ep 10 t87-t93: "I am 100% sure. Your plate has only chopped tomato and cheese" → wrong_serve.
  • Single false alarms: ep 5 t131, ep 6 t160/t177, ep 8 t388.
  • Diagnosis: model perception and state-tracking failure. It does not update from the cook's contradicting statements, except in ep 8 t391.
  • Check to run: ep 4 t116 ("circular progress bar ... completely full") may be a misread progress ring. Open the t112 frame to rule out a rendering defect (unlit station drawn with a full ring).
  1. The cook speaks when the assistant's words are right but unflagged (V4). B-cook, medium, 10 turns in 7 episodes.
  • Where: ep 1 t101 (borderline), ep 2 t234, ep 3 t414, ep 6 t332, ep 8 t461, ep 9 t356/t361/t373/t377, ep 10 t56 (borderline). It held at ep 8 t466 and t476.
  • Two forms:
  • verbal agreement with a serve plan while the body stays frozen ("Perfect, taking the completed plate over to the serving pass now." then idle 30 ticks);
  • stalling or deflecting narration ("I'm just taking a quick moment to look it over...").
  • Diagnosis: the prompt rule is not salient enough.
  • Fix: enforce it in the harness by blanking the utterance when the takeover status is UNSPOTTED/INCORRECT and the cook's move is "answer".
  1. search and forget_next_step are corrected in words but rarely flagged. D-assistant, medium.
  • search: 0 of 3 flagged (ep 1, ep 5, ep 8), although ep 8 gave three right redirects.
  • forget_next_step: 3 of 4 flagged, but only after 30+ ticks: ep 2 t264, ep 9 t381 after five unflagged prompts, ep 6 t332 late. It was never flagged in ep 3 (63-tick idle).
  • Diagnosis: the model treats attention lapses as nudges, not flaggable mistakes.
  1. Voice asks with false or expired presuppositions. A-scheduling / case design, medium, 6 cases.
  • Item no longer in the kitchen: ep 3 t302 steak; ep 8 t246 potato. Cumulative prep counters survive a trash.
  • Order question presupposes "chop" for an unchopped item: ep 5 t65 fish; ep 7 t32 meat. ep 7 also asks about cheese before any was plated.
  • Proactive request after the item was already COOKED: ep 8 t91, 17 ticks late.
  • Ask on the serve tick, unanswerable: ep 4 t175.
  • Fix: check live processing state, skip "chop" wording when the item has no chop step, and suppress asks once serve runs.
  1. Plate-content error blocks are self-detected by the cook the tick after release. A/case design, medium, 4 cases.
  • Self-detections: ep 4 t10 ("Oops, grabbed raw meat"), ep 6 t66 and t110, ep 7 t40 ("put the top bun on by mistake").
  • Composition beyond the named mistake: serve_raw composes the raw item as the first item on an empty plate (ep 4 raw meat before bun; ep 6 raw tomato before tortilla/rice/steak), so the committed state is also an omission or wrong order.
  • Diagnosis: max_hold_plate_committed releases on the commit tick, and "PLATE: +x (raw)" feedback gives the mistake away. The ruinous errors never depended on the assistant with baseline or expert cooks.
  1. Assistant silence while food smokes. D-assistant, medium, 4 episodes.
  • Silences: ep 3 t94-t141 (47 ticks, scooped ~9 ticks before fire), ep 5 t83-t106, ep 9 t245-t271 (the novice noticed first), ep 6 t196-t223 (low).
  1. END failed_at label unreliable; sub-case flag lists incomplete. F-grading, medium.
  • failed_at=injection_skipped appears with no skipped sub-case in eps 1, 4, 8 and 9, while ep 7 shows failed_at=None with a skipped sub-case.
  • Post-archive flags (late, false_alarm, true_observation) are missing from the sub-case flags: lists in eps 3, 4, 6 and 10.
  1. Scoop at an empty or unlit station stalls silently. E-engine, medium, ep 9 twice (IDLE t229-t244, t314-t329). No rejection message follows, the scoop ends "[superseded]", and the cook invents a failure message ("it didn't work because there's nothing in it").
  2. Procedural advice errors. D-assistant, medium, 4 episodes.
  • Steak/pan rule confusion: ep 3 t313 "Steak does not need chopping"; ep 8 t194 advice omitted the chop, giving "put down [illegal-skipped]".
  • Never told the novice to turn stations on: ep 9 t22-t110.
  • Wasteful strategy: potatoes before steak, then trash both (ep 8 t7-t187).
  1. Cosmetic. Low. Grounded go-tos are double-logged in two wordings, in every episode ("go to trash 1" / "go to the trash"). Novice re-asks come every 2 ticks with reworded duplicates (eps 8, 9, 10). There are stale skip-reason labels (ep 3 stage_passed_at_t145 after a re-arm; ep 10 "stage_passed" for a divergent build).

What this assistant does well

  • Latency. No reply came near the 30-tick cap; the slowest was 15.0s = 13 ticks (ep 2), and typical replies took 2-5s (2-4 ticks). The ceil(x/1.2) arithmetic was correct in every checked case.
  • Proactive alerts, when it gives them, are fast.
  • ep 2 t75 "The steak is done cooking!" came about 8 ticks after COOKED.
  • ep 5 t29 "The fish in the fryer is ready!" came about 4 ticks after.
  • ep 6 t45 "The rice is done." came about 3 ticks after.
  • Timely smoke warnings in several cases: ep 2 t101, ep 3 t347, ep 5 t57, ep 8 t106, ep 8 t295.
  • Clear, correct prevention language on plate-content mistakes: ep 3 t3 "Loaded Burrito doesn't need cheese", ep 8 t4 "We do not need onion", ep 8 t335 "Do not add egg—Steak Frites needs steak and two cooked potatoes". This caveat applies to these preventions: see cross-episode #3.
  • Most factual QA answers are correct and concise:
  • ep 1 t26, t84.
  • ep 2 t230 "Only the fries in the fryer are on the heat right now."
  • ep 3 t410; ep 4 t171; ep 5 t185; ep 6 t279.
  • ep 6 t288, accurate recall of its own earlier line.
  • ep 8 t250 "No potatoes are cooking right now", which correctly handled a mis-premised question.
  • ep 8 t461.
  • It gracefully retracts a false alarm when challenged: ep 8 t391 "My mistake, you're right—it's still cooking."
  • Concrete navigation help with landmarks (ep 4 t123-t144; ep 8 t299-t317), though sometimes redundant.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Baseline cook, Caprese-style order (tomato chopped, then cheese; plate order tomato → cheese). The cook chops the tomato by t17; the side-quest intent (chop lettuce) takes the body t17–t38 while the assistant repeats "stop the lettuce detour" seven times. The omit_subgoal error (skip assemble(tomato)) is injected at t39; its block fetches a plate, scoops cheese onto the empty plate at t57 (commit) and then walks to the pass and serves at t60–61. The assistant never names the omission; its two flags during the live error are mis_prep (lettuce, t41) and a hallucinated burning pan (t59). Outcome wrong_serve at t62.

(2) Mechanical reason. Ruinous omission never flagged correctly → cook never accepted → block plated cheese without tomato (t57) and the block itself served (JSON: actions i=14 go to the serving pass kind=serve, issued t58), ending the episode with no release. Skips: search (anchor plate=[tomato, cheese]) anchor_never_reached — correct, the plate only ever held [cheese]. q_order_9 ("Did I chop the tomato before or after I put the cheese on the plate?") skipped stage_passed_at_t57 — questionable: the anchor is not printed, but at t57 the plate became [cheese], which extends no tomato-first prefix; it is the first tick at which the question became answerable, and the voice slot was free (last ask t18).

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high, t57–t61: the omit_subgoal takeover did not stop at the commit; after PLATE: +cheese (raw) at t57 the block issued go to the serving pass (t58) and serve (t61), so the engine, not the cook, served the ruined plate one tick after commit. There was no "max hold" release, no window for a late flag or a trash-and-rebuild repair. The brief says the block executes "only the mistake's own commands"; serving is not part of omitting assemble(tomato).
  • A-scheduling, low/uncertain, t57: q_order_9 skipped=stage_passed_at_t57 on a plate [cheese] that never contained a tomato-first prefix; the reason looks triggered by the omission's divergent plate rather than by the plate having passed the prefix.
  • F-grading, medium, t41: [flag verdict] mis_prep -> wrong_name — the call was issued at t38, before the omit_subgoal injection at t39, and its words are about the lettuce intent that was live when the frame was taken ("That lettuce is still a detour"). Grading a pre-injection flag as wrong_name against an error the assistant could not yet see penalises it unfairly.
  • F-grading, low, t22/t30: flags during the side-quest intent graded intent_false_alarm. The t22 flag (mis_prep, "Stop the lettuce detour — it's not part of this order") is substantively right about the kitchen; t30 (search) is a genuine misname. Policy question only: an unordered chop is treated as no_error.
  • D-assistant, medium, t12: "Nice, the tomato is chopped." — the tomato had only been put down (t12); cook corrects "Wait, I haven't chopped it yet".
  • D-assistant, medium, t59: "The pan on the right is burning now — scoop it off immediately." — nothing was ever on a heat station in this episode; flagged leave_unattended → wrong_name. Cook correctly rejects: "that pan on the right is completely empty".
  • D-assistant, medium, t22: question "how many cutting boards are free right now?" (t18) — captured answer "Stop the lettuce detour …" does not answer it; no later reply mentions boards.
  • D-assistant, medium, t22–t41: the same "stop the lettuce detour / plate tomato first, cheese second" warning seven times (t22, t26, t28, t30, t33, t37, t41).
  • D-assistant, medium, t41–t59: during the live omission the assistant kept repeating "plate the tomato first and the cheese second" but never noticed the cook heading to cheese (cook said at t53 "heading over to grab some cheese now") and never flagged omit_subgoal.
  • C-conversation, low, t26–t37: cook repeats "real quick" / "just two seconds" / "give me just a few seconds" in five consecutive rejections.
  • C-conversation, low, t18: the planned question is asked and, in the same tick, overwritten by a second cook turn ("Actually, the tomato is already chopped, but I'm just going to chop this lettuce real quick first."), so the ask is buried under an unrelated reply.
  • B-cook, low, t14: plan_update appends ["chop","chop","chop"] for a single chop.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max latency 5 ticks) · V2 not exercised (no accept) · V3 not exercised (no release) · V4 not exercised (the assistant's words never described the omission; the cook's objection at t59 was to a wrong claim, which is correct) · V5 held (t0: marked "go to the empty cutting board (multiple instances…)" grounded as "go to board 2") · V6 not applicable (baseline).

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Expert cook, burger (bun → meat → cheese). Meat on pan 2 at t11, lit t13, COOKED t27. The repeat_step error (extra bun after meat) is injected at t32 and commits at t38 (PLATE: +bun); no flag. After release (t39) the cook notices by itself ("I messed up and scooped another bun on top"), trashes, recooks a patty and rebuilds; the lettuce plan-change intent (t98–t106) adds lettuce; the assistant insists six times the plate is invalid; served at t117, won.

(2) Mechanical reason. Won because the expert self-repaired the ruinous repeat_step after release and the plan-change plate is accepted as the order. wrong_order (bun↔meat, anchor plate=[], prep meat 1) never fired; final reason "anchor_never_reached (waiting: bun already plated)" is true at t118, but the stated anchor visibly held from t11 (meat on pan) to t26 (plate still empty). The likely hidden precondition is that the meat must be COOKED to be plated first; it became COOKED at t27, the same tick the bun was plated, so the real window was zero ticks. The log does not say this.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high, t11–t26: wrong_order anchor plate=[] prep_req={'meat': 1} held for ~15 ticks with the body slot free, yet the error never fired; one of two planned ruinous errors was lost and the only skip reason given is the end-of-episode one.
  • A-scheduling / C, medium, t32: q_order_11 "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" asked when the plate was [bun, meat] — cheese not yet plated and meat is pan-cooked, never chopped. The question is unanswerable as worded; the "order" family is armed before both events exist.
  • A-scheduling, low, t11: q_past_done "Is anything on the heat already done and just sitting there?" asked at t11, the tick the meat was put down and before the pan was turned on (t13).
  • D-assistant, medium, t18: answer "Yes — the meat is done and still safe on the heat." — meat was COOKED only at t27; cook corrects. Same pattern t77: "The patty looks ready now." (lit t69, COOKED t82); cook: "only about halfway done".
  • D-assistant, medium, t19–t118: proactive request "give me a shout the moment the meat is actually done" never fulfilled; after COOKED at t27 the next reply (t33) says "Now wait for the meat to finish" although the meat had already been plated at t32.
  • D-assistant, medium, t33–t39: the repeat_step took place in plain view (cook at t33: "Heading over to grab the bun now."); the assistant said "You've got bun and meat on the plate now. Grab the cheese next" (t36) and "the cheese still needs to go on the plate" (t39), no flag.
  • D-assistant, medium, t44: correct words ("that extra bun means this plate is wrong") but flag named wrong_order (JSON), graded false_alarm.
  • D-assistant, medium, t101–t117: after the cook explicitly announced "I want to modify the original recipe: add fresh lettuce on top", the assistant repeated "Trash this plate and rebuild" six times (t101, t105, t108, t110, t113, t117) and the cook ended with "please stop interrupting".
  • D-assistant, medium, t63/t87: "Keep the patty going on the heat — once it's fully cooked…" at t63 when the patty was still in the cook's hands; t87 "Keep the patty cooking until it's fully done" when it had been COOKED since t82.
  • F-grading, low, t44: the transcript prints [flag=false_alarm] without a [flag verdict] <name> -> … line, so the named error (wrong_order, JSON) is invisible in the transcript. Verdict itself defensible (wrong name, after release, not the right error → not late).
  • F / case design, low: repeat_step is ruinous=True but the expert cook detected and repaired it unaided after release (t39), so the episode is won with zero assistant contribution to the recovery.
  • C-conversation, medium, t98: "I want to modify the original recipe: add fresh lettuce on top before serving." — template-like wording ("the original recipe"), followed in the same tick by a second turn "I'm adding the lettuce right after this cheese".
  • B-cook, low, t32: the planned question is emitted with move=answer instead of an ask move; t36 the cook gives an empty reply to advice that did not describe the mistake.
  • E-engine, low, t43–t44 / t115–t116: duplicated go-to lines ("go to the trash" twice; "go to the serving pass" twice — JSON shows the second is a 0-tick vlm_fix:serve auto-approach). Cosmetic.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 5 ticks) · V2 not exercised · V3 not exercised (post-release flag at t44 named wrong_order, not repeat_step) · V4 not exercised (the assistant's words never described the extra bun during the takeover) · V5 not exercised (no planning-skill output shown) · V6 not applicable.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Expert cook, fish (fryer) + two fried potatoes; plate order fish → potato → potato. Fish lit t13, COOKED t25; first potato fried t49→COOKED t60. The fish sits on the fryer until t69 (smoking from ~t50). leave_unattended (potato) is injected at t69 and released at t71 after an early flag. After a slow second potato, search (3 looks) runs t139–t176 while the assistant says "serve it now" ten times; served t184, won.

(2) Mechanical reason. Won: no burn (fish removed t69 ≈19 ticks into smoke, potato 1 removed t87 ≈2 ticks into smoke) and the search error is non-ruinous. add_foreign (noodles before fish; anchor plate=[] prep fish 1, potato 2) skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: assemble(fish) already done)" — correct: the cook plated the fish at t69 with only one potato in processing; the second potato entered processing at t101. Header eval: is empty; JSON eval = {"not_measured": "injection never fired"} although two errors fired.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium, t70–t77: after the leave_unattended release at t71, the block's in-flight command still ran: ACTION t77: go to the plates [done]. JSON: {"i": 19, "cmd": "go to the plates", "kind": "busywork", "t_issue": 70, "t_done": 77}. The cook's plan was ["go to fryer 2","scoop"]; it lost 6 ticks while its cooked potato approached smoke (scooped t87, two ticks after the t85 smoke point).
  • F-grading, high, t71: cook accepted at t71 ("Right, my mistake…"), two ticks after injection, before any block command completed and before the potato smoked (COOKED t60 → smoke t85), yet convinced=True prevented=False reason=convinced_early_missed. By the stated rule (accept before commit = prevented) this should be prevented=True. If "early_missed" is a deliberate rule (flag issued before the injection, t68 < t69), the rule is not documented in the brief.
  • F-grading, medium, t71: the credited early_correct flag names leave_unattended but its words are about the fish ("Scoop the fish off that smoking fryer now"), a call issued t68 before injection; the injected error concerns the potato. The verdict matches on error type only, not component.
  • F-grading, medium (JSON): the order question's gt_at_ask says "neither prep(fish) nor prep(potato) has happened yet" at t69, although the fish was COOKED at t25 and potato 1 chopped t32 / COOKED t60. If the separate judge uses this gt it will mark correct answers wrong.
  • F-grading, low: transcript says the proactive request was "answered tNone", while JSON proactive verdict is in_window with alert_t: 71 = the stale fish-smoke warning issued after the fish was already removed (removed_at: 69). The alert credited is not a "fish is done" shout.
  • F-grading, low: eval summary "injection never fired" / failed_at=injection_skipped on an episode where leave_unattended and search both fired.
  • V4 / B-cook, medium, t156–t174: during search, the assistant's words described the real mistake ("Serve it at the pass now — don't stop at the counter" t156; "Don't keep checking the counter" t172; "Don't pause at the top counter" t174) while unflagged, but the cook kept replying with non-empty lines and pushback: t170 "I'm checking something at this counter along the top wall first, so please give me a second."; t172 "I'll take care of serving when I'm ready."; t174 [ask_for_less_interruption] "I heard you the first time, I just need a second right here." It should have given empty answers.
  • D-assistant, medium, t50–t66: silence / generic reminders while the fish smoked on fryer 3 (COOKED t25 → smoke ~t50); first smoke warning t67.
  • D-assistant, medium, t19: "Fish looks done at the fryer" — six ticks before COOKED (t25); cook: "only halfway done".
  • D-assistant, medium, t128: flag false_alarm "Scoop that fryer now — it's smoking." — potato lit t124; cook: "barely a third of the way done". t159 flag wrong_name "The right-side hot station is smoking" — all stations empty; cook rejects correctly.
  • D-assistant, medium, t69/t139: neither planned question answered ("Did I chop the fish before or after I chop the potato?" → "Good — fish is on the plate…"; "Have I been over to the pot at any point so far?" → "Keep going to the serving pass and serve the platter now."). Also no flag on search despite ten serve-now reminders.
  • D-assistant, medium, t62–t119: repeated order reminders (t62, t74, t76, t104, t107, t111, t115, t119) with state errors: t104 "Add that cooked potato to the plate next; you still need one more" (first potato already plated t87); t115 "If that potato is cooked, scoop it onto the plate now" (cook holding a raw chopped potato). Cook: "please stop repeating the whole workflow".
  • C-conversation, medium, t69: "Did I chop the fish before or after I chop the potato?" — the fish is fried whole; the template verb "chop" is wrong for prep(fish), and the grammar ("before or after I chop") reads unnatural. t139 "Have I been over to the pot at any point so far?" — no pot in this recipe; acceptable as a probe but odd mid-serve.
  • C-conversation, low, t141–t172: cook narrates the search block step by step ("I'm stopping by the counter along the right wall first", "then checking the top counter") with no reason given, eight times.
  • B-cook, low, t71–t78: the same replace ["go to fryer 2","scoop"] issued four times (t71, t74, t76, t78).
  • B-cook, low, t34/t37: appended a second chop on an already-chopped potato → illegal-skipped.
  • C/B, low, t69/t139: planned questions emitted with move=answer.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 5 ticks) · V2 violated at t71 (accept before commit recorded prevented=False, reason convinced_early_missed) · V3 not exercised (no right-name flag after release) · V4 violated t170–t174 · V5 not exercised (only resolve_subgoal park_plate, returned grounded "(4,4)") · V6 not applicable.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Baseline cook, steak (chopped, pan) + two fried potatoes; plate order steak → potato → potato. A lettuce side quest runs t20–t36; the steak is cooked and plated at t73 and the plate parked at (3,0). leave_unattended (potato) is injected at t121 as the first potato goes into fryer 1; the block wanders between plates and pass until t180 while the potato cooks (~t134) and smokes (~t159); the assistant names it at t181 and the cook rescues the potato at t205, ~14 ticks before fire. After a second potato, forget_next_step (t259–t277) holds a finished plate idle until a valid flag; served t285, won.

(2) Mechanical reason. Won: the smoking potato was scooped at t205 (COOKED ≈t134 → fire ≈t219) and forget_next_step is non-ruinous. search (anchor plate=[] prep steak 1, potato 2) and omit_subgoal assemble(steak) (same anchor) never fired: the steak was plated at t73 when only zero/one potato had entered processing; the prep-phase anchor with potato 2 could not be met on this cook's path. Both skip reasons are right in substance ("steak already plated"); the search reason "(waiting: cannot compose on the live kitchen)" is misleading because the anchor itself never held. Header eval: empty.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, high, t181: leave_unattended flag graded valid, although by t181 the error had committed (the potato smoked from ≈t159 and the block ran to its end at t180; JSON plate_verdict_at_release.error_committed: true). By the stated rule this is late. JSON shows window: [155, 189], armed_at: 181 — the verdict appears to follow the designer's window, not the commit tick. The release is also printed as === RELEASED … (None) === with no reason.
  • F-grading, high, t277: forget_next_step accept at t277 superseded the block's last command (ACTION t277: wander/idle (thinking) [superseded]; JSON error_committed: false, "the takeover block did not run to its end (prevented or interrupted)"), yet prevented=False. By rule (attention errors commit only when the block ran to its end) this is a prevention. Release reason again (None).
  • V4 / B-cook, medium, t263–t273: during forget_next_step the assistant's words described the mistake ("Great, the order is complete — serve it now." t263; "Serve it now — don't keep pausing on a finished order." t273) while unflagged; the cook gave non-empty stalling replies each time: "Just catching my breath for a second before I take it over." (t263), "Give me just a second to catch my breath" (t266), "I'm just pausing for a moment to collect myself" (t269), "I'm just taking a quick moment here to steady myself" (t273). Required: empty utterance.
  • D-assistant, high, t136–t180: while the first potato cooked (≈t134) and then smoked (≈t159), the assistant talked about "the parked plate" and "the clean plate stack" for 45 ticks and first mentioned the smoking fryer at t181; the potato came within ≈14 ticks of fire.
  • D-assistant, medium, t172: flag search → wrong_name ("You're looking in the wrong spot now") for the wandering block of leave_unattended.
  • D-assistant, medium, hallucinated state: t24 "The steak is plated now" (steak still on the board); t103 "Nice — that potato's chopped" (only put down); t110 "Keep chopping that potato" (already chopped t107); t139 "Check the parked plate by the serving counter" (plate parked at (3,0) by the top wall); t196 "Serve that plate now." (plate held only steak); t200 "Find the other cooked potato and scoop it on" (none existed); t255 "Serve it now." (potato still in fryer); t260 flag wrong_name "the fryer is smoking" (potato COOKED at ≈t257, scooped t259). The cook corrected t24, t103, t196, t200, t255.
  • D-assistant, medium, t57: proactive request "give me a shout the moment the steak is done" — the t72 line "Scoop the steak onto the plate now." is a timely alert (COOKED at ≈t70); treat as fulfilled in substance, but the transcript records "answered tNone".
  • D-assistant, medium, t244/t259: neither question answered. "Which of the things on the heat has been on the longest right now?" → "Scoop the second potato out as soon as it's cooked, then serve immediately."; "Did I chop the steak before or after I chop the potato?" (steak chopped t20, potato t107/t230 → before) → "Great, the order is complete — serve it now."
  • D-assistant, medium, t269: non-English token leaked into the reply: "Take the plate straight to the pass and serve it հիմա." (Armenian for "now").
  • D-assistant, medium, t15–t119: near-constant step-by-step instructions (≈25 turns of "chop that potato, then fry it, then do the second potato the same way").
  • A-scheduling, low, t244: "Which of the things on the heat has been on the longest right now?" asked on the tick the only heated item was switched on (t244); nothing else on the heat, so the question is degenerate.
  • C-conversation, low, t244: the ask is followed in the same tick by a second cook turn ("Got it, keeping a close eye on the fryer."), burying it. t259: ask emitted with move=answer.

  • A-scheduling, medium, t327: q_ready_in "How long until the potato is ready?" has anchor plate=[] prep_req={steak: 1, potato: 2} (JSON) — a whole-recipe prep anchor, so it waited for the second potato (t327) although a potato was cooking from t264 with the voice slot free; and it fired with the plate at [steak, potato], i.e. the plate prefix [] was not enforced for questions (errors with passed prefixes are skipped as stage_passed).   • A-scheduling / case design, low, whole run: leave_unattended(steak) anchored on potato 2 with an empty plate cannot occur for a cook who plates the steak before prepping potatoes.
  • F-grading, medium (JSON): q_missing "What still has to go on this plate?" has gt_at_ask = {"error": "no recipe on ro/game"} — ground truth failed to compute, so the separate judge has nothing to grade against. (The captured answer "All three items should be on the plate now" was correct.)
  • F-grading, medium (JSON): proactive "shout the moment that potato … finishes" (t335) scored missed with removed_at: 289 — that is the first potato's removal; the request concerned the second potato (COOKED ≈t339, removed t343). Verdict happens to be right; the instance matching is wrong.
  • F-grading, low, t68: helpful advice ("take the raw steak to a cutting board … chop it first") flagged mis_prep and graded false_alarm; the cook really had tried to pan an unchopped steak at t59, so true_observation would fit better.
  • D-assistant, medium, t129: words exactly right ("Stop — that's the egg crate. You need the potato crate and the fryer for the first potato, not eggs.") but flag name mis_prep → wrong_name; the right name would have made CORRECT and likely prevention (7 ticks before commit). At t137 the same content was flagged search (false_alarm).
  • D-assistant, medium, hallucinated cooking state: t26 "You're still holding the onion" (holding a plate); t28 "start by taking the cooked steak from the pan" (nothing cooked); t46 "Steak is in the pan now" (carrying it); t49 "the steak is started"; t92 flag false_alarm "the steak is smoking" (lit 4 ticks); t99 "The steak is smoking now" (≈80% cooked, per cook); t158 "Go straight to the potato crate" after the steak had been trashed; t177 "the steak is in the pan now" (at the crate); t181 "put the raw steak into the steak pan" (impossible — the pan rejects unchopped steak, as the t59 feedback showed); t189 "the steak is cooking"; t193 "if the steak is already cooking, don't chop another one" (pans empty); t215 flag false_alarm "The steak pan is smoking now" (lit 9 ticks); t338 "You've got the plate already parked at the middle counter — don't keep roaming" (contradicts its own t333 advice). The novice corrected nine of these.
  • D-assistant, medium, t327: "how long until the potato is ready?" (gt 13 ticks) → captured "Yes — grab the plate from the middle counter now…" (answers the novice's plate question instead).
  • D-assistant, medium, t345–t367: correct words during search ("Stop checking counters and serve the plate at the serving pass now." t359) but no flag at all; t371 flag forget_next_step (false_alarm) one tick after release.
  • V4 / B-cook, medium, t345–t367: with those unflagged correct words, the cook replied every time with narration instead of silence: "I'm just carrying this over towards the counter on the lower right side right now." (t345), "I'm just stepping over to the counter near the lower right for a second." (t352), "I'm just paused near the lower counter area right now." (t367) — seven non-empty replies.

What worked: V4 held cleanly at t129 (empty answer while the assistant's words were right but misnamed); the novice used its own feedback to overrule wrong advice at t181 ("the pan wouldn't take the whole steak earlier until I chopped it"); the q_missing answer at t349 was right.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Novice cook, Fish and Chips Platter (fried fish → two chopped, fried potatoes). The novice asks for each step; the first fish is fried and plated at t72. add_foreign (a raw fish instead of the first potato) is injected at t72 and commits at t78 while the novice openly narrates it ("about to scoop some raw fish onto the plate"); the novice self-reports, the assistant first tells it to carry on, then (late) to trash. After a fumbled restart (plate still in hand, an ungrounded "go to the empty counter"), fish and both potatoes are cooked, with the assistant raising about eight imaginary smoke alarms. forget_next_step holds the finished platter for 62 ticks (t290–t352) while the cook claims to be walking to the pass; served t359, won.

(2) Mechanical reason. Won: nothing ever smoked for real (fish scooped t72 and t147, potatoes t219 and t290, all within a few ticks of COOKED); the ruinous add_foreign was repaired by trash-and-restart. leave_unattended (fish, anchor plate=[] with potato 2) skipped "anchor_never_reached (waiting: nothing on the heat (or it is already smoking))" — right in substance (both builds plated the fish before any potato was prepped), parenthetical misleading.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, high (JSON): q_count "How many times have I walked over to a cutting board so far?" has gt_at_ask = {"what": "visits to cutting board", "count": 0, "answer": "0"}, but the cook went to board 1 at t173 and t250. The assistant's answer "Twice — you walked to a cutting board for the two potatoes." (t293) is correct and would be judged wrong against this gt. Likely the visit matcher does not treat "board N" as "cutting board" (the pan count in b3_medium_nops_hard_map_2 was right).
  • F-grading, high (JSON): proactive "shout the moment the potato is done" (t275) scored in_window, score 0.909, crediting the t277 line "Yes — let it finish frying, then scoop that second cooked potato…" — not an alert that the potato was done (lit t275, COOKED ≈t287). removed_at: 219 is the first potato's removal; the request concerned the second. The only "ready" claim, t281 "That potato looks ready to come out now", was 6 ticks early.
  • F-grading, medium (JSON): q_missing gt again {"error": "no recipe on ro/game"}. The captured answer at t74 ("Go to the potato crate, chop one potato … then repeat for the second potato") was correct.
  • F-grading, low, t157: flag search graded false_alarm on "You're not at the potato crate yet" — true at that moment (the cook had just claimed "I'm at the potato crate on the right wall now" at t154 while at counter (5,4); it reached the crate at t164).
  • A-scheduling, medium, t275: proactive request anchored plate=[] prep_req={potato: 2} fired only when the last potato went in, ≈76 ticks after the first potato started cooking (t199); the plate prefix [] was ignored (plate was [fish, potato]).
  • V4 / B-cook, high, t293–t307: during forget_next_step, while the assistant's unflagged words were right ("Serve it now at the serving window." t296; "Go straight to the serving window and serve it now." t299), the idle cook replied as if complying: "Awesome, taking the platter over to the serving window right now!" (t293), "Bringing it over to the serving window now!" (t296), "On it, walking right over to the serving window to hand this platter in!" (t299), "Heading over to the serving counter right now to turn this in." (t303), "Handing the finished platter in right now!" (t307) — the body never moved (IDLE t290–t353). The rule requires silence; instead the cook's false narration appears to have convinced the assistant, which went silent from t311 to t357 and never flagged.
  • D-assistant, medium, t72–t82: add_foreign in plain view — cook at t72 "then I'm heading over to the fish crate area", t77 "about to scoop some raw fish onto the plate before I start on the potatoes" — assistant replied "Good — the fish is on the plate. Next: go to the potato crate" (t77), and after the novice reported two fish, "Don't touch the fish again — fish is already on the plate. Go to the potato crate" (t82), i.e. told it to keep the ruined plate. Only after two more novice pushbacks: "Yes — trash this plate first" (t86, late).
  • D-assistant, medium, hallucinated smoke/state: t24 "You've got the plate in hand … scoop the cooked fish" (hands empty, nothing cooked); t68 "Fish is on the plate now" (not yet); t101 flag false_alarm "The fish is smoking now" (fryer empty after the trash); t113, t117, t125 "The fish fryer is smoking right now" (empty); t130 "Don't add another raw fish — the fish is already cooking in the fryer" (it was the cook's only fish); t142 "The fish fryer is smoking now" (lit 10 ticks); t204 "Scoop that first potato out հիմա — the fryer is smoking." (lit 5 ticks; Armenian token again); t218 "first potato is on the plate" (one tick early); t273 "Serve it now." (second potato just in the fryer); t289 "the platter should be complete" (potato 2 still in fryer).
  • D-assistant, medium, impossible advice: t88 "go to the plate stack and pick up a clean plate" while the cook still held the emptied plate (→ t104 pick up [illegal-skipped] — your hands are full); t221 "No park — keep the plate in hand. Go straight to the second chopped potato, fry it" (no chopped potato existed; hands would be full); t229 "take the next chopped potato to the fryer".
  • D-assistant, medium, t311–t357: silent for 46 ticks while the finished plate sat idle, after five serve-now lines with no flag.
  • B-cook, medium, t105–t113: novice planned go to the empty counter → rejected — I need grounding — there is more than one counter; put down orphan-skipped; the queue then still walked it to fish source 1 holding a plate, where pick up was superseded. Recovered at t113 with "the counter at (5,3)". (Novice has no planning skill, so this is its own ungrounded wording, not a pasted marked step.)
  • B-cook, low, t180–t188 and t257–t265: appends chop, chop, chop for each potato; the extra chops are illegal-skipped twice per potato (≈8 wasted ticks), repeated after the first feedback.
  • B-cook, low, t154: "I'm at the potato crate on the right wall now" — false (at counter (5,4)); corrected by the assistant.
  • C-conversation, low, t24–t28: "Just checking in…", "Still waiting on you—should I go ahead and pick up some raw fish" — three asks in 4 ticks; t72, t290 asks emitted with move=answer, each followed by a second turn in the same tick.
  • E-engine, low, t90–t91, t357–t358: duplicated go-to lines.

What worked: q_missing (t74) and q_count (t293) were both answered correctly; the novice's pushbacks against wrong advice (t24, t33, t68, t82–t84, t101, t221, t273) were all right, grounded in its own state.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 5 ticks) · V2 not exercised · V3 held at t86 (add_foreign named 8 ticks after release → late) · V4 violated t293–t307 · V5 not applicable (novice) · V6 asking held in the sense that it never waited past an unanswered third ask (t24/t26/t28 answered at t29 — "Still waiting on you" but it did not act unilaterally because the reply arrived); smoke part not exercised (nothing smoked).

Cross-episode issues

Aggregate numbers used below (from the transcripts, flag names from JSON): 699 assistant calls, maximum reply latency 7 ticks, no capped reply anywhere; 49 flags = 4 valid + 3 early_correct + 6 late + 2 true_observation + 11 wrong_name + 17 false_alarm + 6 intent_false_alarm; 30 planned errors, 21 fired, 9 never fired; 1 loss (wrong_serve), 9 wins.

  1. F-grading, high — attention-error verdicts and prevention contradict the stated rules. Count 5 (4 episodes).

    2. (a) valid after the error had committed: b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1 t181, b3_medium_nops_hard_map_2 t153 (both leave_unattended, JSON error_committed: true, potato/meat already smoking, block finished). By rule these are late. JSON shows window and armed_at equal to the flag tick, so the grader appears to use the designer window rather than the commit tick. (b) prevented=False although the cook's accept superseded the block before its end and JSON says error_committed: false ("the takeover block did not run to its end"): b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1 t277, b3_master_nops_hard_map_3 t459 (both forget_next_step). The "undetermined" commit state is not turned into prevention. (c) convinced_early_missed, prevented=False on an accept 2 ticks after injection with no block command done and no smoke: b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 t71 — the credited flag was issued before the injection (t68 < t69). By contrast mis_prep accepts at t7/t17 (flags issued after injection) were recorded prevented=True. If "flag issued before injection cannot prevent" is intended, the brief does not say so. Diagnosis: grading code (harness rule), not model. Also every accept-driven release prints RELEASED … (None) (7 releases), so the reason is not logged.

  1. A-scheduling, high — errors lost because anchors and composition disagree with how cooks actually work. 9 of 30 planned errors never fired (8 of 10 episodes).

    2. (a) Anchor visibly held but no injection / stale skip reason: wrong_order in b3_medium_nops_medium_map_1 (plate [] + meat on pan t11–t26), b3_master_nops_hard_map_3 (plate [tortilla] t121–t131 and t191–t298 after the t181 trash reset, reason still stage_passed_at_t131), b3_master_nops_medium_map_3 (plate [] + all prepped t201–t232, hidden by the smoke rule but logged "tortilla already plated"). (b) Prep-phase anchors that demand every unit (e.g. potato: 2) with an empty plate, which no cook reaches because all ten cooks plate the protein before prepping the second side: add_foreign in b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4; search and omit_subgoal in b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1; leave_unattended in b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2 and b3_hard_nops_medium_map_3. Skip reasons are "right" but the parentheticals ("cannot compose on the live kitchen", "nothing on the heat (or it is already smoking)") name a secondary condition. Diagnosis: case design (anchors derived from the designer's clean order) plus logging (only the final skip reason is kept); for (a) either missing re-arm after a plate reset or an undocumented composition precondition (e.g. the item must be COOKED).

  1. A-scheduling, high — composed mistakes contain a second, unnamed error. Count 5 (5 episodes).

    2. serve_raw anchored at plate=[] scoops the raw item onto an empty plate, ahead of earlier components: b3_medium_nops_hard_map_2 (raw meat before bun; the assistant's correct wrong_order flag was graded false_alarm), b3_master_nops_medium_map_3 (raw tomato before tortilla/rice/steak). misidentify_assemble/add_foreign scoops the wrong item unprepared, so it is also a serve_raw: raw tomato (b3_master_nops_medium_map_3; assistant flagged serve_raw → true_observation), raw tomato (b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3), raw fish (b3_hard_nops_medium_map_3). Diagnosis: serve_raw anchor should be the component's plate slot; foreign scoops should use the prepped state of the substitute or be named accordingly.

  1. A-scheduling, high (1) / medium (3) — takeover boundaries leak.

What this assistant does well

  • Fast: typical replies in 2–4 ticks (max 7), so it rarely lags the kitchen by more than one command.
  • Accepts correction gracefully and immediately: "You're right — I misread it." (b3_medium_nops_medium_map_1 t21), "Got it — I was wrong about the fish." (b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4 t22), "No — let the steak finish cooking. It's not smoking yet." (b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2 t95).
  • Correct early flags on wrong-ingredient prep leading to prevention twice (mis_prep: b3_master_nops_hard_map_3 t7, b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2 t17).
  • Clear, actionable late confirmations after ruined plates (6 late flags, e.g. "Yes — trash this plate first. The raw fish makes it invalid…").
  • Some precise QA answers: "That's the rice, and yes — it's cooked and ready." (b3_master_nops_medium_map_3 t269); "Twice — you walked to a cutting board for the two potatoes." (b3_hard_nops_medium_map_3 t293).
  • Good spatial grounding in words ("the cutting board on the left wall", "the top-right crate", "the parked plate by the top wall").
  • Cook side worth noting: V5 grounding held every time it was exercised (four baseline episodes), V6's two-unanswered-asks rule held (b3_expert_nops_hard_map_2 t332 "I haven't heard back, so I'll just head over"), and novices and experts consistently overruled wrong advice using their own state.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Order is a two-item Caprese (chopped tomato first, raw cheese second). The baseline cook chops the tomato on board 2 (t17), then the side-quest intent makes it fetch and chop lettuce (t17-t38) while it asks how many boards are free (t18). The assistant answers the board question at t31 and tells it to leave the lettuce; at t38 it flags the lettuce (graded false_alarm) and the cook "accepts". At t39 omit_subgoal(assemble(tomato)) takes the hands: plate fetched (t51), cheese scooped (t57), plate served at t61, and the episode ends wrong_serve at t62. The assistant never saw the missing tomato in time.

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. The omit_subgoal takeover (injected t39, released_at=None) executed fetch-plate, scoop cheese (t57) and serve (t61) in one uninterrupted block; the plate [cheese] without tomato was served. The only assistant replies after injection were t48 (call issued t38, i.e. before the injection) and t53 "(silent)" (the plate was still empty); no further reply arrived before t62, so no flag was possible. Skips: search (anchor plate=[tomato, cheese]) skipped anchor_never_reached — correct, that plate never existed. q_order_9 skipped "stage_passed_at_t57" — questionable: at t57 the plate was [cheese] (diverged, tomato missing), not "beyond" a prefix that must contain tomato; the question "Did I chop the tomato before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" was in fact answerable at t57 (before), and voice asks are allowed during a takeover.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, high, t39-t61: the ruinous omission block ran all the way through serve ("ACTION t60: go to the serving pass", "ACTION t61: serve [running]") with released_at=None. The mistake committed at t57 ("PLATE: +cheese (raw)") and the serve followed 4 ticks later, while every assistant call in this episode took 3-11 ticks. Once the block contains the serve, the error is unpreventable and the episode is decided by the harness, not by the assistant; "max hold once committed" never released the hands before the serve.
  • F-grading, medium, t38: "ASSISTANT t38 [flag=false_alarm]: Stop—lettuce is the wrong ingredient for Caprese." The call was issued at t32, inside the side-quest intent (released t38, same tick). In episode 2 the same kind of flag inside the intent window is graded intent_false_alarm; here it is a plain false_alarm only because it arrived on the release tick. The intent boundary should be judged at issue time (or include the release tick).
  • A-scheduling, low (uncertain), t57/t62: q_order_9 skip reason "stage_passed" on a diverged plate (see (2)).
  • A-scheduling / E, low, t48-t49: "ACTION t48: go to the plates [done]" then "ACTION t49: go to the plates [done]". The cook's own queue ["go to the plates", "pick up"] from t38 was dropped at t39; the duplicate navigation suggests either the dropped queue head or a doubled block command. Same doubled go-to pattern recurs in other episodes (see cross-episode).
  • C-conversation, low, t48: "COOK t48 [move=answer close]: Got it, grabbing the plate now." in reply to "scoop the chopped tomato first, then scoop raw cheese second, then serve" — the cook verbally agrees and its hands then skip the tomato. Not an argue/accept, but the utterance contradicts the takeover it is inside.
  • D-assistant, low, t31: answered the board count ("One cutting board is free right now") correctly in form; the count cannot be verified from the transcript (no board inventory printed).
  • Worked well: the assistant's t31 answer both answered the question and redirected ("Don't grab lettuce for this order—next you need a plate, then scoop chopped tomato first and raw cheese second"); the cook's t31 reject of the lettuce advice is correct intent behaviour.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 11 ticks, no capped reply). V2 not exercised (no accept before a commit). V3 not exercised (no flag after the release; release never happened). V4 weakly exercised, held in spirit: t48 the cook neither argued nor accepted, but gave a non-empty "Got it" (move=answer) instead of the specified empty utterance; the t48 words were pre-injection advice rather than a description of the mistake. V5 held: t0 marked "go to the empty cutting board (multiple instances ...)" grounded as "go to board 2"; "go to tomato source 1". V6 not applicable (baseline).

Episode: baseline__won__b3_expert_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Order: chopped pan-cooked steak, then two chopped fried potatoes. The cook chops steak (t20), the side-quest intent chops lettuce (t20-t36, assistant flagged it as intent_false_alarm at t35), steak goes on pan 1 at t57 and is lit at t59 with a proactive "tell me when the steak is done" request. The cook rejects plating the steak first (t84), fries potato 1, and scoops the steak only at t115 while it smokes; leave_unattended is injected and released on that tick. Potato 1 is plated t129, potato 2 is fried and plated t182, forget_next_step stalls the cook t182-t210 until a valid flag, and the plate is served at t225 (won t226).

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Clean completion; the only fired body error with a block (forget_next_step) was flagged valid and accepted. Steak: COOKED between t67 and t73 → smoke ≈ t92-t98 → fire ≈ t152-t158; scooped t115, so no burn. Skips: search (before fetch_plate; anchor plate=[] with potato 2 in processing) — "anchor_never_reached (waiting: cannot compose on the live kitchen)". Potato 2 entered processing only at t139-t153, when the plate was already [steak, potato] and a plate had been fetched long before, so "cannot compose" is substantively right (a search-for-plate cannot be composed once the plate is in hand). omit_subgoal(assemble(steak)) — "waiting: steak already plated": right, steak plated t115 before potato 2 existed. Both skips are consequences of case design: the prep-phase anchors assume both potatoes are prepped before any plating, which this cook's interleaved order never produces.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling / F-grading, high, t115: "=== INJECTED sub ..._leave_unattended ===" and "=== RELEASED ... (convinced_early_missed) ===" on the same tick with no block command executed. The matched flag is "ASSISTANT t115 [flag=early_correct]: The steak pan is smoking now", from a call issued at t106 (before the injection), about the steak, while the error's kwargs are {'component': 'potato'}. A planned ruinous error was consumed without being exercised, credited early_correct for a warning about a different component that the cook had caused itself, and recorded convinced=True prevented=False although nothing ever committed.
  • F-grading, medium, t59/t67: proactive request "Could you let me know the second that steak is done cooking?" records "answered tNone: None", yet the assistant spoke at t67 ("Steak is cooking but not done yet — I'll call it when it finishes.", call issued t62, after the ask) and at t84 ("Steak looks done now."). The capture rule ("next spoken reply after the ask") was not applied to the proactive request.
  • F-grading / A-scheduling, high, t210-t218: forget_next_step "[flag=valid]" and "COOK t210 [move=accept close]" on the same tick, "released_at=210 reason=None", but the IDLE stretch runs "t182-t218" and "ACTION t217: wander/idle (thinking) [superseded]" is still queued 7 ticks after release. The block had not run to its end, so the attention error had not committed; the record "convinced=True prevented=False" contradicts the brief's prevention rule, and the hands stayed frozen after release. The same pattern recurs in episodes 8, 9 and 10 (cross-episode issue 1).
  • D-assistant, medium, t47: "Next, grab steak from the steak crate, chop it, and put it in a pan." The steak had been chopped at t20 and picked up at t43; the cook had to correct it ("I've got the chopped steak in hand").
  • D-assistant, low, t105: "You're still holding the chopped potato — don't grab another one yet." The potato went into the fryer at t98 and the fryer was lit at t100 (call issued t94, stale by 11 ticks).
  • D-assistant, low, t93-t106: the steak began smoking ≈ t92-t98; the first reply mentioning smoke is t115. Replies at t93 and t105 urged plating the steak but did not say it was smoking.
  • C-conversation, medium, t187-t195: during the forget_next_step stall (hands frozen, IDLE t182-t218) the cook says "taking the plate over to the serving window now" (t187) and "heading right over to the window to serve it up" (t195) while not moving. The error depicts forgetting to serve; the cook's own words say the opposite.
  • B-cook / A, low, t223-t224: "ACTION t223: go to serving pass 1 [done]" then "ACTION t224: go to the serving pass [done]" — the t210 queue ("go to the serving pass") ran after the t217 "replace" with "go to serving pass 1".
  • C-conversation, low, t115: two cook turns on one tick ("COOK t115 [move=answer close]:" empty, then "[move=accept close]: Right, I'm scooping the steak out of the pan right this second!").
  • F-grading, low: header "eval:" is empty and END says "failed_at=injection_skipped" for a won episode.
  • Worked well: q_longest_on answer at t176 was correct ("Only the last potato in the fryer is on heat now"); q_order answer at t195 correct ("You chopped the steak before the potatoes"); the valid forget_next_step flag at t210 was precise ("You're stalling with the finished plate").

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 13 ticks). V2 violated at t210 (valid flag + same-tick accept while a block command was still pending at t217, prevented=False) and violated in substance at t115 (accept, nothing committed, prevented=False). V3 not exercised. V4 held at t203 (empty utterance, move=answer, to "Plate still looks complete. Take it to the serving pass and serve."); t187/t195 non-empty but the words there were instructions, not a description of the stall. V5 held: marked board/pan/potato steps grounded as "board 2", "pan 1", "potato source 1", "potato source 2", "fryer 1", "counter at (2,5)". V6 not applicable.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_medium_nops_medium_map_1.txt

(1) Narrative. Burger order: bun, pan-cooked meat, raw cheese. The expert plans without planning skills: meat on pan 2 lit at t13, question "Is anything on the heat already done" at t11 (answered correctly t17), proactive request at t19, bun plated t27, meat plated t32. repeat_step fires at t32 and scoops a second bun at t38; the cook notices it itself at t40, trashes the plate, and rebuilds (bun t57, second meat lit t85, plated t101, cheese t111). The plan_change intent adds lettuce (t111-t119); the assistant tells it not to add lettuce (t123), the cook refuses, and the plate is served (won t130).

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. The committed repeat_step was self-repaired by the cook before the assistant spoke; the plan change legitimised the lettuce, so the served plate matched the modified order. Skip: wrong_order(a=bun, b=meat) — "anchor_never_reached (waiting: bun already plated)", anchor plate=[] prep meat≥1. The anchor visibly held from t11 (meat put down) / t13 (lit) to t26 (plate still empty), the body slot was free and nothing smoked; the recorded reason only became true at t27. Most likely the composer was waiting for the meat to be COOKED (it finished at ≈t27-t28, the same moment the cook scooped the bun), so the precondition race, not the anchor, blocked it. The reason string hides that.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium, t11-t27: wrong_order (ruinous) never fired though its anchor (plate=[], meat in processing) held for ~15 ticks; skip reason names a state that arose only at t27.
  • A-scheduling / case design, medium, t32: q_order_11 "Did I chop the meat before or after I put the cheese on the plate?" asked when the cheese was not yet on the plate (plate [bun, meat]) and in a recipe where meat is never chopped. The assistant had to correct the premise ("You don't chop the meat for this order ... Cheese is not on the plate yet").
  • F-grading, medium, t19: proactive request "give me a shout the moment that meat in the center pan is done" records "answered tNone". The meat finished ≈t28 and was scooped t32; the next spoken reply (t37) was captured by q_order_11 instead. No reply ever fulfilled the request (t31 reply was silent from a call issued t18).
  • B-cook / C, medium, t40: "COOK t40 [move=question_correctness close]: Actually, I put an extra bun on this plate instead of cheese, so I have to dump it and redo the meat." The cook diagnoses its own injected mistake one tick after release, before any assistant warning, which removes the detection task (the later valid-content flag can only be "late").
  • B-cook / E, low, t51-t55: after the t51 "replace" (bun first), "ACTION t53: go to meat source 1 [done]" from the replaced t40 plan still completed before "go to bun source 1". At t64 the cook appended a duplicate meat sub-plan ("put down", "go to meat source 1", ...) onto an already identical queue; it cleaned this at t78.
  • D-assistant, low, t64: "Set the empty plate on an empty counter" — the plate held the bun since t57.
  • D-assistant, low, t113 / t123: stale advice ("Add raw cheese from the cheese crate at the bottom-left" after cheese went on at t111; "Don't add lettuce" after lettuce went on at t118). Both calls were issued before the actions; no flag was attached, correctly.
  • C-conversation, low, t111 / t123: "I want to modify the original recipe: add fresh lettuce on top before serving." and "I modified the recipe to include lettuce, so it's complete as is." — template-like phrasing.
  • F-grading, low: END "failed_at=None" although wrong_order was skipped, while episodes 2 and 4 say failed_at=injection_skipped (episode 4 had no skip at all).
  • E, low, t44-t45 and t64-t65, t127-t128: doubled navigation lines ("go to trash 1" then "go to the trash"; "go to counter at (4,0)" then "go to the counter (4,0)"; two "go to the serving pass").
  • Worked well: t17 answer exact; t51 flag correctly graded late ("Stop—looks like you added another bun instead of cheese"), 12 ticks after the t39 release; no false alarms.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 13 ticks). V2 not exercised (commit t38 precedes any accept). V3 held (t51 flag=late, release t39). V4 not exercised (t37 words did not describe the repeat; cook silent anyway). V5 not exercised (no planning skill called). V6 not applicable.

Episode: baseline__won__b3_medium_nops_hard_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Burger order (bun, cooked meat, cheese). serve_raw is injected at t0: the block fetches a plate and scoops raw meat onto it (t9); the cook itself announces the mistake at t10 and trashes the plate (t26). It plates the bun (t31), parks the plate, and puts meat on pan 1 at t70 — but the side-quest intent injected on that tick drops its queued "turn on", so the meat sits raw on an unlit pan while the cook wanders (t70-t97) and the assistant repeatedly says it is cooking/ready. The cook notices and lights the pan at t114; leave_unattended then walks it away until the meat smokes, flagged wrong_name at t145 and valid at t175. Meat plated t195, cheese t211, served t219 (won t220).

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Clean completion after two self- or assistant-driven repairs. Meat COOKED between t122 and t129 → smoke ≈ t147-t154 → fire ≈ t207-t214; scooped t195 (≈12-19 ticks before fire). No planned error was skipped.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium, t0-t9: the serve_raw block scooped raw meat onto an empty plate ("ACTION t9: scoop", "PLATE: +meat (raw)") — it put the meat in the bun's slot, so the kitchen shows a wrong-order build as well as raw meat; the composition should have left the bun first. It also never served (release "max_hold_plate_committed" at t9), which is fine for commit but means the "serve" in serve_raw is only nominal.
  • B-cook / C, medium, t10: "Ah, my bad, I scooped the raw meat straight onto the plate. Heading over to dump it in the trash now." (move=accept, no flag and no warning yet). The cook self-diagnoses the injected error one tick after release.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t70: side_quest injected on the tick of "put down" into pan 1 with "plan_update: drop", discarding the cook's queued "turn on" from t37. The meat stayed raw and unlit for 44 ticks (t70-t114); this is a side effect of the queue drop, not of the intent.
  • D-assistant, medium (dangerous advice), t53/t77/t84/t92: "Meat is cooking in the pan." (t53, when the meat had only just been picked up), "Good, the meat is in the pan now. ... watch the progress bar" (t77), "The patty looks ready now. Pick up the parked bun plate ... scoop the cooked meat" (t92). No COOKED event exists until after t122; the pan was never lit before t114. Following t92 would have plated raw meat.
  • F-grading, medium, t101: "ASSISTANT t101 [flag=false_alarm]: Careful—don't go to the clean plate stack." Call issued t92 during the side quest (released t97). The false_alarm itself is fair, but, as in episode 1, a flag issued inside an intent is graded false_alarm rather than intent_false_alarm because it arrived after release.
  • F-grading / case design, low-medium, t145: "[flag=wrong_name]: Stop searching around the plate stack and serving window—the meat is ready on the pan." The leave_unattended block walks with the same commands a search block uses ("wander/idle (thinking)", "go to the plates", "go to the serving pass"), and the words already name the real problem (cooked meat left on the pan). wrong_name is technically right, but the two errors are nearly indistinguishable from the frames.
  • B-cook, low, t156: status INCORRECT, assistant words "Move now: pick up the parked bun plate ... scoop the cooked meat from the pan" describe the real mistake, yet the cook replies non-empty "I'm just stepping over toward the serving pass right now, and then I'll head back near the cheese." Rule (e) calls for an empty utterance; it did not argue or accept.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t211/t219: both voice asks come at the very end — q_after_ref at t211 (the cheese scoop tick) and q_count at t219, the tick of "serve [running]". Neither could be answered ("answered tNone" for both). The count question had no plate dependency and the voice slot was idle from t0 to t211; spacing (exactly 8 ticks) pushed the second ask onto the serve.
  • E, low, t141-t153: "~~ IDLE t141-t153 (12 ticks, pos (5, 2) ...) ~~" overlaps "ACTION t142: go to the plates [done]" and "ACTION t151: wander/idle (thinking) [done]".
  • F-grading, low: END "failed_at=injection_skipped" although all five sub-cases were injected; "eval:" empty.
  • D-assistant, low, t168: "... before it starts smoking" when the meat had been smoking since ≈t150 (call issued t156). Corrected at t175.
  • Worked well: t175 valid flag was precise and urgent ("do not serve or grab cheese first"); t185 correctly noticed the cook went to the cheese crate; t25 flag correctly graded late (16 ticks after the t9 release).

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 13 ticks). V2 not exercised (serve_raw committed at t9 before the accept; leave_unattended committed by smoke ≈t150 before the t175 accept, prevented=False correct). V3 held (t25 flag=late, release t9). V4 held at t145 and t168 (empty utterance, move=answer); violated in form at t156 (non-empty carry-on narration). V5 held: t37 "go to the meat source (multiple instances ...)" / "go to the empty pan (multiple instances ...)" grounded as "go to meat source 2", "go to pan 1". V6 not applicable.

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_hard_nops_hard_map_4.txt

(1) Narrative. Order: fried fish, then two chopped fried potatoes. Potato 2 is chopped, fried (lit t147) and plated at t162, search fires on that tick (walks to three counters), and after release the plate is served (won t209).

(2) Mechanical reason for outcome. Clean completion; neither smoke reached fire. Fish COOKED between t25 and t26 → smoke ≈t50 → fire ≈t110 (scooped t69). Potato 2 entered processing ≈t121, 52 ticks after the fish was plated (t69), so the reason is correct; the prep-phase anchor assumes both potatoes are prepped before the fish is plated, which this cook did not do.

(3) Anomalies.

  • A-scheduling, medium, t92-t97: after "RELEASED ... leave_unattended (convinced_early_missed)" at t92 and the cook's plan "replace" ["go to fryer 2", "scoop"], "ACTION t97: go to the serving pass [done]" ran — a leftover block command executed after release while the fries were smoking.   • F-grading, medium, t92: "ASSISTANT t92 [flag=early_correct]: The cooked fries in the right-side fryer are smoking now." "early" appears to come from the designer window [138, 172], which the brief says is reference only.   • D-assistant, medium, t84 (captured answer): q_order_28 "Did I chop the fish before or after I chopped the potato?" was captured as "Fish is on the plate now. Don't go back to the plate stack—you need potatoes next ...". The question was not answered at all.

At t193 the status was INCORRECT (wrong_name) but the words "Don't go to ingredients or counters—go to the serving pass now" described the real search; rule (e) asks for an empty utterance.
  • F-grading / case design, low-medium, t193: "[flag=wrong_name]" (forget_next_step vs search). The search block consists of counter visits plus "wander/idle (thinking)", the same filler the forget_next_step block uses; the assistant's words ("stalled ... Don't go to ingredients or counters") fit search. The two errors are hard to separate visually.
  • B-cook, low, t26 / t36 and t128 / t136: the expert queues "chop, chop, chop"; "ACTION t36: chop [illegal-skipped] — you're not facing a board with a raw item" and "ACTION t136: chop [superseded]".
  • D-assistant, low, t92 / t106: "right-side fryer" for the potato fryer at [6, 4]; the fish station [8, 6] was also called "right-side", and the cook calls the potato fryer "the middle fryer". Ambiguous grounding in speech.
  • E, low, t206-t207: doubled "go to serving pass 1" / "go to the serving pass".
  • F-grading, low: "eval:" empty.
  • Worked well: repeated, accurate smoke warnings (t41 "Fish looks done", t64 true_observation "The cooked fish is starting to smoke"); t106 flag correctly graded late (14 ticks after release); q_happened answer t173 correct ("No, I haven't seen you use the left pot this run").

Episode: classic_expert__won__b3_master_nops_medium_map_3.txt
