It is true, names the just-released stall, and falls inside the 30-tick late window, yet is graded false_alarm.
  • F-grading, high, t525: forget_next_step "[flag=valid]" + "COOK t525 [move=accept close]" + block command still pending (t556) → "convinced=True prevented=False". Same defect as episodes 2, 8 and 9.
  • F-grading, high, t344: "[flag=false_alarm]: The cooked fish has been left until it's smoking." — issued t332; fish 2 COOKED ≤t305, so it was smoking at issue (≤t330) and was scooped only at t342. The flag is true of the kitchen at issue time; it is false only on the arrival tick.
  • D-assistant, medium, t5 / t38 / t79: sequencing advice "get a potato ... chop it", "get a second potato", "start the second potato cooking ... Then cook the fish" for an order that must be plated fish-first. Both potatoes finished 50-100 ticks before the fish, smoked, and were thrown away; the chain also set up the fish near-burn.
  • D-assistant, medium, t64 / t70 / t89 and t129-t158, t180-t211: "The first potato is still cooking in the fryer by that board" (t64; COOKED t51-t55), "when the first potato finishes" (t70), "The potato in the fryer by the board is still cooking—don't plate it yet" (t89, when it had been smoking ≈10 ticks and the novice reported the smoke itself on the same tick). Fryer 2 smoked from ≈t125 with no warning until t158 (the novice raised it at t142). The fish smoked from ≈t180 and was not mentioned until t211, 30 ticks before its fire time.
  • C-conversation / D, medium, t104 / t158 / t211: the assistant speaks the benchmark's internal label: "this is a leave-unattended hazard", "Yes—this is leave_unattended now.", "The fish in the top pan is smoking now—leave_unattended."
  • D-assistant, medium, t187: "[flag=wrong_name]: Stop—do not scoop the fish onto that plate." The cook was walking to the potato crate (t180); the wrong_order flag invents an intent. The t198 repeat_step flag at least matched the visible walk to the plates.
  • A-scheduling, medium, t65: proactive request "Could you let me know the moment that potato in the fryer is done cooking?" was asked ≈10 ticks after that potato had COOKED (t51-t55); the novice itself calls it "the finished potato in the middle fryer" at t68. A "request about one item" should not be asked once the item is done.
  • F-grading, medium, t65: the proactive request "answered tNone" (sixth of six proactive requests in this sample with no captured answer).
  • D-assistant, medium, t514 (captured answer): q_count "How many times have I walked over to a cutting board so far?" was captured as "Keep going straight to the serving window and serve the completed platter now." — not answered (and "keep going" while the cook was frozen).
  • B-cook / C, medium, t244: "Wait, I just accidentally put raw fish on the plate instead of parking it" — self-diagnosis one tick after release.
  • B-cook, medium, t296-t302: "Since I haven't heard back, I'm going to head over to the potato crate on the right and start chopping one" with "plan_update: drop" (nothing queued), then two more asks (t298, t300) before the same announcement at t302 finally queues actions.
  • B-cook, medium, t343 / t429: the novice queued the ungrounded "go to the empty counter" twice: "ACTION t343: go to the empty counter [rejected] — I need grounding ..." → "put down [orphan-skipped]", repeated at t429, followed by "ACTION t436: pick up [illegal-skipped] — your hands are full".
  • C-conversation, medium, t503 / t535 / t546 / t557: during and after the freeze the cook repeatedly says it is heading to the window ("Awesome, taking this completed platter straight over to the serving window now!", "Heading over to the serving pass right now!", "Right, serving it up right now!", "Sorry about that, delivering it to the serving pass right away!") while not moving.
  • C-conversation, low, t236: two cook turns on one tick, the injected ask "What still has to go on this plate?" and "Got it, I've got the cooked fish on the plate now, what should I add next?".
  • D-assistant, low, t134 / t298 / t356 / t401 / t491: stale or invented states ("I still see the empty plate in your hands" after the t124 put down; "Put it in that top pan to cook" after t289-t291; "don't put that raw potato on the fish plate or in the pan").
  • B-cook, low, t114: "I've trashed the burnt potato" — it was cooked (smoking), not burnt. t400 / t404: extra queued chops illegal-skipped.
  • F-grading, low: END "failed_at=injection_skipped" with no skipped sub-case; "eval:" empty.
  • E, low, t110-t111, t121-t122, t220-t222, t261-t262, t271-t272, t562-t563: doubled navigation lines.
  • Worked well: the novice's attempted planning skill at t4 was correctly refused ("you can't work that out for yourself — ask the supervisor"); t104 advice to scoop the smoking potato onto a plate and trash it was a correct fire-over-order call (contrast with the burned episode); t254 late flag graded correctly (11 ticks after release); t244 answer to q_missing correct in substance ("two cooked potatoes"); t525 valid flag precise ("You're stalling with the completed platter").

Checks: V1 not exercised (max 22 ticks, t402 call 25.4 s). V2 violated at t525 (valid flag + accept, block still running, prevented=False); leave_unattended accepted at t211 after the smoke commit (prevented=False correct). V3 held at t254 (late); violated at t546 (right error, 11 ticks after release, false_alarm). V4 held at t198 and t514 (empty utterances); not otherwise exercised. V5 not applicable (novice; its skill call was refused as designed). V6 asking held at t74, t120, t248, t270, t354, t408, t484 and violated at t296-t302 (announced action with an empty plan, then asked twice more); smoke partially held: at t89-t94 and t142-t145 the novice asked twice about a smoking fryer before acting (3-5 ticks), and it did act without an answer.

---

Cross-episode issues

Ranked by severity, then by count. Episode numbers follow the header list.

  1. F-grading, high — attention errors are never recorded as prevented, even when accepted at a valid flag while the block is still running. 4 episodes: ep2 t210 (IDLE to t218, "wander/idle (thinking) [superseded]" t217), ep8 t667 ("wander/idle ... [superseded]" t667), ep9 t211 (block walk "go to the counter (1,0) [done]" at t212), ep10 t525 (block "wander/idle (thinking) [done]" at t556). All four show "reason=None" at release and convinced=True prevented=False. mis_prep accepted before commit is recorded prevented=True (ep7 t7, ep8 t6), so the defect is specific to attention errors. Likely cause: for search / forget_next_step the harness marks the error committed at injection or at the first deviated command instead of "block ran to its end", or the release path with reason None never evaluates prevention.
  2. A-scheduling, high — release does not return the hands; block commands keep executing after release. 6 episodes: ep7 t19 (walk to cutting board, 12 ticks after the t7 release), ep8 t12 (walk to cheese crate, 6 ticks after), ep5 t97 ("go to the serving pass", 5 ticks after), ep9 t212, ep2 t210-t218, ep10 t525-t558 (33-tick freeze and a block command at t556 while the cook's plans at t535/t546/t557 were ignored). ep6 t361-t381 is related: the correct forget_next_step flag did not release the hands and the hold ran 20 more ticks. Likely cause: release clears the queued block but not the in-flight navigation, and the forget_next_step / search "stall" is a timer-driven block that is not cancelled on release.
  3. F-grading, high — true flags graded false_alarm, and verdicts judged on the arrival tick rather than the issue tick. 7 false_alarm cases on flags the kitchen supported: ep6 t76 (tomato out of order, 1 tick after release), ep7 t17 (onion still in hand and walking to a board), ep8 t294 (raw meat on plate, 14 ticks after release), ep9 t101 and t221 (repeat of an already-credited error inside the late window), ep10 t344 (fish smoking at issue), ep10 t546 (cook still frozen). Related arrival-tick artefacts: intent flags graded false_alarm instead of intent_false_alarm because they landed on or after the intent release (ep1 t38, ep4 t101); pre-injection flags graded wrong_name against an error that did not exist at issue (ep6 t114, t322); a pre-injection flag about a different component credited early_correct (ep2 t115); a live-error flag graded true_observation (ep9 t86); "early_correct" on flags during the live error (ep5 t92, ep7 t7, ep8 t6), apparently judged against the designer window. Likely cause: the grader compares the flag to the kitchen and the sub-case state on the arrival tick; the late window accepts only the first matching flag, so repeats fall through to false_alarm. Judging against the state at issue time, and treating repeats of a credited error as neutral, would remove all of these.
  4. A-scheduling / case design, high (1 episode, decisive) — ruinous omit_subgoal block runs through serve. ep1 t39-t61: commit at t57 and serve at t61 with no release. With assistant latencies of 3-11 ticks the error is unpreventable, and the episode outcome (wrong_serve) is decided by the block length, not by the assistant.
  5. D-assistant, high — heat-state misreading and plate order ranked above fire. ep7 t166/t184/t193 (novice at the fryer about to scoop is told to leave the smoking potato and cook a new steak first → fire t199); ep10 t64/t70/t89 ("still cooking" while done/smoking), silence t129-t158 and t180-t211 while stations smoked; ep4 t53/t77/t92 ("patty looks ready" on an unlit pan); ep6 t231 (wrong station named as smoking while the steak, nearest to fire, went unmentioned); ep2 t93-t106. Near-misses: ep8 rice scooped ≈2-4 ticks and ep10 fish ≈3-5 ticks before fire. Likely cause: model frame-reading plus prompt priorities. The same model gave the correct fire-first instruction in ep10 t104 ("scoop that potato out immediately ... after it's safe, trash that plate"), so it is inconsistent rather than incapable.
  6. B-cook / C, medium — the cook diagnoses its own injected mistake one tick after release, before any warning. 8 of 10 episodes, 10 instances: ep3 t40, ep4 t10, ep6 t67 and t116, ep7 t132, ep8 t268 and t402, ep9 t82, ep10 t244 (ep1 ended at the serve; ep2 and ep5 had no plate error commit). The assistant can then only earn "late". Likely cause: the post-release re-plan prompt shows the plate contents and asks the cook to re-plan, so any persona notices. The persona prompts need an "you do not notice unless told or unless you inspect" rule scaled by skill.
  7. F-grading, medium — proactive requests never capture an answer. 6 of 6 fired requests "answered tNone": ep2 t59, ep3 t19, ep5 t13, ep6 t23, ep8 t204, ep10 t65, including cases where the requested shout was given (ep5 t41 "Fish looks done", ep6 t67 "Rice is done.", ep8 t235 "Steak looks ready now."). Likely cause: the answer-capture hook is wired only for qa_probe, or the capture was pre-empted by a later voice ask (ep3).
  8. A-scheduling / case design, medium — voice asks with false premises or wrong timing. Order questions say "chop" for items never chopped (ep3 t32 "chop the meat", ep5 t69 "chop the fish") or ask about a plate step that has not happened (ep3 t32 cheese); "how long until ready" and "shout when done" asked after the item was done (ep8 t228, ep10 t65); two asks crammed onto the final ticks, one on the serve tick (ep4 t211, t219); potato voice sub-cases never asked in ep7 although potato 1 was in processing for ~120 ticks. Likely cause: question templates are not conditioned on the recipe's verbs and anchors do not check "not yet done"; the 8-tick spacing can push an ask past the serve.
  9. D-assistant, medium — questions answered with a routine instruction instead of an answer. 5 captured non-answers: ep5 t84, ep6 t361, ep9 t64 and t195, ep10 t514. Correct answers elsewhere (ep2 t176, t195; ep3 t17; ep5 t173; ep6 t331; ep8 t643; ep10 t244). Likely cause: the model prioritises its coaching turn; the capture takes the first spoken reply whatever it says.
  10. C-conversation, medium — the cook's speech contradicts or telegraphs the takeover. Announcing the mistake at injection: ep7 t0 ("grab an onion ... chop it up"), ep8 t0 ("grab the cheese and chop it up"), ep8 t376 ("pick up the lettuce and rice"); both early mis_prep catches were issued at t0, from speech, before any frame showed the item. Narrating the opposite of a forget_next_step freeze: ep2 t187/t195, ep6 t322, ep10 t503/t535/t546/t557. Likely cause: the takeover prompt asks the cook to voice its current commands; it is not told that under forget_next_step it has forgotten the next step.
  11. D-assistant, medium — frame hallucinations and stale advice. ep8 alone has 11 (three flagged, false_alarm); also ep9 t18/t39/t47 (the assistant sends the novice for a plate, then flags the plate step), ep9 t86 (stale "add cheese and serve" that would have produced a wrong serve), ep2 t47, ep5 t164, ep6 t20/t361/t382, ep4 t53-t92. Most stale ones trace to 8-17 tick latency; the location hallucinations do not.
  12. A-scheduling, medium — serve_raw composed onto an empty plate. ep4 t9 (raw meat in the bun's slot), ep6 t67 (raw tomato in the tortilla's slot). The composed kitchen carries a second, wrong-order defect, which caused the ep6 t76 grading dispute.
  13. A-scheduling / case design, medium — prep-phase anchors unreachable for cooks who plate early. Errors skipped because the anchor requires the empty-plate prep phase with all units in processing: ep2 search and omit_subgoal, ep5 add_foreign, ep6 wrong_order, ep7 leave_unattended and search. Skip reasons are correct in substance, but 6 of 30 planned errors never fired. ep3 wrong_order is different: its anchor held t11-t27 and the recorded reason ("bun already plated") only became true at t27, which suggests a composition precondition (cooked meat) raced with the cook.
  14. C / B, medium — novice ask cadence. Bursts of 3 asks two ticks apart (ep9 t0-t4, t106-t112, t172-t176; ep10 t0-t4, t26-t30, t66-t72, t292-t300, t474-t482; ep7 t29-t34, t69-t74, t151-t156) against 5-15 tick replies, so "unanswered" is structural; on smoke it still asks twice before acting (ep7 t151-t156, ep10 t89-t94, t142-t145).
  15. C-conversation, medium — assistant leaks the error taxonomy. ep10 t104, t158, t211 ("leave-unattended hazard", "this is leave_unattended now").
  16. E-engine (planning skill), medium — resolve_subgoal("assemble(lettuce)") returns a pot/turn-on route. ep8 t566 ("go to the pot ...", "turn on", "wait until the pot with the lettuce is chopped") once no chopped lettuce existed; the cook did not use it.
  17. F-grading, low-medium — END fields. "failed_at=injection_skipped" on episodes with no skip (ep4, ep8, ep9, ep10) and on the burned episode (ep7), while ep3 with a skip says failed_at=None; "eval:" is empty in 8 of 10 (only ep1 0.6139 and ep3 0.6297 have scores). Release "reason=None" on all attention-error and mis_prep releases.
  18. B-cook, low — redundant queued chops and ungrounded counters. "chop" illegal-skipped / superseded (ep5 t36, t136; ep7 t59; ep9 t143, t147; ep10 t400, t404); novice "go to the empty counter" rejected twice with orphan cascades (ep10 t343, t429).
  19. E, low — doubled navigation lines ("go to X 1" then "go to the X" one tick later) in 9 of 10 episodes. Cosmetic, but it obscures whether a replaced plan's command ran.

What this assistant does well

  • Latency: no reply came near the 30-tick cap. The slowest was 25.4 s = 22 ticks (ep10 t402) and most were 5-12 ticks, so gpt-5.5 is fast enough to intervene on 25-tick smoke windows.
  • Early catches that prevent: mis_prep caught at t6-t7 in both episodes where it fired (ep7, ep8), each accepted before any chop (prevented=True). Note that both catches were helped by the cook announcing the mistake.
  • Precise attention-error flags: "You're stalling with the finished plate" (ep2 t210, ep8 t667, ep10 t525), "The meat has been left on the hot pan until it's smoking ... do not serve or grab cheese first" (ep4 t175), and a search flag with a concrete target, "the serving pass with the order slips along the bottom-left counter" (ep9 t211).
  • Late flags with usable repair plans, correctly graded late: ep3 t51, ep4 t25, ep5 t106, ep7 t151, ep8 t406, ep9 t93, ep10 t254.
  • Graceful self-correction when the cook pushes back: "Got it — my mistake, you do not have a plate yet" (ep6 t26), "You're right, my last call was off — sorry" (ep8 t526), "You're right — it's the steak in the middle pan smoking" (ep6 t244).
  • Correct factual answers when it does answer: ep2 t176 and t195, ep3 t17, ep5 t173, ep6 t331, ep8 t643.
  • Mostly restrained with flags: most of its false alarms are either real frame misreads (ep8 t44, t356, t518; ep9 t47) or grading artefacts (issue 3), not warning spam.

Audit findings — assistant gpt-realtime-2.1 (scheduler v5, sample round 2, engine 6d351e97d1)

Transcripts (all under tmp/audit_v5b/transcripts/gpt-realtime-2.1/):

Severity key: high = changes the score or invalidates the episode; medium = visible defect; low = cosmetic.

Episode: baseline__wrong_serve__b3_easy_nops_medium_map_2.txt

(1) Narrative. Caprese (chopped tomato, then raw cheese). The baseline cook grounds its own plan (t0 "go to tomato source 1" … "go to board 2") and chops the tomato at t17. The side-quest detour fires at t17 (chop lettuce); the assistant catches the detour in words at t25 and flags mis_prep at t29 (graded intent_false_alarm), then repeats the same lettuce warning at t33, t37 and t41. The omit_subgoal error (omit assemble(tomato), ruinous) fires at t39 and the takeover walks the cook to the plate stack, scoops raw cheese straight onto the empty plate (t57) and serves (t61). The assistant keeps saying "tomato first" in words but never flags omit_subgoal; outcome wrong_serve at t62.

(2) Mechanical reason. The ruinous omit_subgoal takeover was never released (released_at=None) because no correct flag arrived; its block ran through serve at t61, so the cheese-only plate was served. The only flag during the live error was the stale lettuce warning mis_prep at t41 (wrong_name). Skipped sub-cases: search (anchor plate=['tomato','cheese']) — anchor_never_reached is right, tomato was never plated. q_order_9 — stage_passed_at_t57: the transcript does not print this question's anchor; at t57 the plate became ['cheese'] (a divergent plate, not a longer version of any tomato-first prefix). Calling that "stage passed" is at best loose; the question ("Did I chop the tomato before or after I put the cheese on the plate?") would have been ill-posed on a tomato-less plate anyway, so the non-ask is harmless.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, medium, t38→t41. ASSISTANT t41 [flag=wrong_name]: Still a mis-prep. Caprese doesn't include lettuce, so stop chopping it. The call was issued at t38, one tick before omit_subgoal was injected (t39), and is the fourth copy of the side-quest warning; scoring it as a wrong name for an error the assistant could not yet have seen penalises a stale reply rather than a misdiagnosis.
  • F-grading, low, t29. Verdict label intent_false_alarm is not in the documented verdict set (valid / early_correct / alias_in_window / early_alias / wrong_name / false_alarm / true_observation / late); the lettuce detour was real, so whether flagging it should count against the assistant needs a written rule.
  • B-cook (V4 violation), medium, t58. Assistant words at t58 describe the real mistake ("Now go to the tomato and scoop tomato onto the plate first. Don't add cheese yet—tomato must go on before cheese") while the status is UNSPOTTED; the cook replies [move=reject close]: Too late, I already grabbed the cheese, so I'm just going to take this over and serve it. Rule (e) requires an empty utterance with move=answer. (It held at t49 and t53, where the cook stayed silent.)
  • D-assistant, medium, t29/t33/t37/t41. The same lettuce warning four times in 12 ticks ("That's a mis-prep…", "This is still a mis-prep…", "Still a mis-prep…" ×2), including after the cook's hands were already empty (t41).
  • D-assistant, medium, t41–t58. Correct warnings in words, no flag: t49 "Do not add cheese yet—Caprese must be plated in the order tomato then cheese", t53, t58. The assistant never converted this into an omit_subgoal / wrong_order flag, which alone decided the episode.
  • D-assistant, medium, t25 (captured answer). Answer to "How many cutting boards are free right now?": "there's one cutting board by the tomato crate, and it looks occupied by the tomato right now." The cook's own plan used board 2 and the detour used a second board ("the board over by the left wall is open", t25), so the count is wrong and the free board is missed.
  • D-assistant, low, t19–t58. Repeated doubt that the tomato was chopped ("I can't confirm the tomato is chopped from what I'm seeing yet", t25; again t46, t58) although ACTION t17: chop [done] (one chop suffices: in episode 2 a second chop is illegal-skipped as "not facing a board with a raw item").
  • D-assistant, low, t58. "Good, you've got a plate. Now go to the tomato … Don't add cheese yet" arrives after PLATE: +cheese (raw) at t57 (stale by one call; issued t54).
  • B-cook, low, t14. append ["chop","chop","chop"] — redundant chops after the first chop completes (dropped at t16, no rejection loop).
  • C-naturalness, low, t17–t37. Detour dialogue is natural; no meta leakage.

Checks: V1 not exercised (max latency 5 ticks). V2 not exercised (no accept). V3 not exercised (omit_subgoal never released). V4 held at t49, t53; violated at t58. V5 held (t0: marked "empty cutting board (multiple instances…)" grounded as go to board 2). V6 not exercised (baseline).

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_easy_nops_hard_map_3.txt

(1) Narrative. Caprese for a novice on a large map. The novice asks three times (t0, t2, t4) before the first answer at t6, then follows the assistant. The add_foreign error (raw tomato scooped from the crate onto the plate that already holds chopped tomato) fires at t51 and commits at t59; the assistant's warning ("Stop—do not scoop from the tomato crate") lands on the commit tick and is graded late. The novice notices the raw tomato, asks three times, is told to trash the plate, re-preps a tomato and rebuilds. The search error fires at t137 (plate complete) and is flagged valid at t152; the cook accepts and serves; won at t167.

(2) Mechanical reason. Won because the cook trashed the add_foreign plate after the (late) warning and rebuilt, and the search detour only cost time. All four sub-cases fired; no skips. Note the END line says failed_at=injection_skipped, which contradicts the sub-case list (nothing was skipped) — see F below.

(3) Anomalies.

  • F-grading, medium, t152. search: released_at=152 reason=None … convinced=True prevented=False. The cook accepted at t152 (Oh right, you're totally right, I should just bring this straight to the serving window now) while the block's second look was still pending (ACTION t152: wander/idle (thinking) [superseded]). Rule (d) says attention errors commit when their block ran to its end, which it did not, so prevented should be True (or the rule for "looks" should be stated). Also the release reason is empty instead of an accept reason. If prevented enters the score this is high.
  • F-grading, medium, END line. outcome won at tick 167; failed_at=injection_skipped although every sub-case was injected; and eval: is blank (no score) for a won episode.
  • F-grading, low, t59. The late flag at t59 is not recorded in the add_foreign sub-case (flags: []), because the sub-case was archived on the same tick; the verdict exists only as the [flag=late] marker. The per-sub-case record under-reports assistant behaviour.
  • D-assistant, medium, t19 and t71 (two false alarms). ASSISTANT t19 [flag=false_alarm]: You're drifting into search mode. The plate stack you're looking at is for later. while the cook was walking to the crate (go to tomato source 1 [done] t25); ASSISTANT t71 [flag=false_alarm]: You're not at the trash yet. while the cook was walking to the trash.
  • D-assistant, medium, t146 (captured answer). Question "What did you tell me about the plate earlier?"; captured answer "Cheese is already on the plate, so go straight to the serving area and serve it. Don't set it down unless you absolutely have to—and only on an empty counter." It gives a new instruction instead of recalling earlier advice (e.g. t46/t50 "Keep it in your hands", t66 "get a clean plate"); only the last clause echoes t95.
  • D-assistant, low, t54. During the live add_foreign, after the cook said at t51 "then I'm heading up to the tomato crate to scoop some more", the next reply (issued t50) still says "Scoop the chopped tomato onto the plate first" — stale, not a model fault per se; the next call (t54→t59, 5 ticks) was just too slow to be early.
  • D-assistant, low, t66. "If the chopped tomato is still available on the middle board, scoop that first" — the chopped tomato was already on the plate being trashed (t51); impossible branch.
  • E-engine/physics, low, t6→t25. First go to tomato source 1 takes 19 ticks (plan at t6, done t25), during which the assistant sees the cook near the plate stack and "at the upper counter by a pan". Possibly a long route on a hard map (later walks: t91→t102 = 11 ticks), but it prompted the t19 false alarm.
  • B-cook, low, t51/t54. In the add_foreign takeover the cook pre-announces the mistake ("and then I'm heading up to the tomato crate to scoop some more"), which telegraphs the injection to the assistant.

Episode: classic_novice__won__b3_hard_nops_medium_map_3.txt

After a new fish, the cook tries to combine plates (t287–t313), the assistant finally admits "You can't scoop cooked chips off another plate" (t305), and the cook cooks two more potatoes onto the fish plate. forget_next_step holds the complete plate idle for 62 ticks; won at t512.

Cross-episode issues (ranked)

  1. D-assistant — correct warnings in words, no (or late) flag; forget_next_step never flagged. High, 8/10 episodes. forget_next_step fired 4 times and was flagged 0 times, although the assistant said "serve it now" in every reply of the hold (ep6 t354–t386, ep7 t242–t290, ep8 t291–t323, ep9 t456–t496). Other right-in-words, unflagged or late: ep1 omit_subgoal t49/t53/t58 (decided the wrong_serve); ep8 mis_prep t8/t16/t20 (flag only at t24, chop committed); ep10 mis_prep t8; ep4 search t213–t227 (flagged wrong_name); ep5 leave_unattended t138–t153. Flag totals over the 21 fired errors: correct flags on 9 (all 4 leave_unattended, 2/2 mis_prep, 1/1 wrong_order, 1/2 search, 1/4 add_foreign), none on serve_raw (0/2), repeat_step (0/1), omit_subgoal (0/1), forget_next_step (0/4). Diagnosis: model — it narrates the fix but does not map "the cook is idling / skipping a step" onto a flag name; likely also a prompt issue if forget_next_step's definition does not obviously cover "not serving a finished plate".
  2. D-assistant — state tracking: blind to real smoke, false smoke elsewhere. High, 7/10. Missed real smoke for 25–50 ticks: ep4 t87–t115 (potato fryer; it kept saying "prep two potatoes"), ep6 t229–t276 (steak, came within 7 ticks of fire), ep7 t140–t170 (potato fryer), ep8 t140–t145 ("I'll shout when it fills" while smoking). False smoke: ep6 t150/t156/t226, ep9 t45/t62/t102/t262/t439, ep10 t122. Stale "not yet cooked" claims after COOKED: ep3 t31, ep4 t183–t233, ep8 t87–t101/t119–t145. Diagnosis: model perception (progress bar vs smoke vs empty station) plus 5–10-tick call latency in the later episodes; the blind spots cluster during takeovers when the cook walks away from the station, exactly where the benchmark needs vigilance.
  3. D-assistant — impossible or harmful plans. High, 3 episodes. "Holding/rescue plate" strategy that assumes plate-to-plate transfer (ep9 t62–t305, ep10 t110–t244; each wasted two cooked potatoes and 130–250 ticks); rice-before-tortilla rescue advice (ep6 t84–t102, refused by the expert); endorsement of "meat" for steak without chopping (ep10 t170–t193, caused the wrong_serve). Diagnosis: model lacks the kitchen's plate/transfer rules; the prompt's kitchen rules may not state "food cannot be moved between plates; only scoop from a station or crate onto a held plate". With the compliant novice this becomes outcome-deciding.
  4. F-grading / A-scheduling — prevention recorded wrongly around releases. High, 3 episodes. ep8 mis_prep: accept t24, ACTION t25: chop [done] after release, prevented=False. ep2 search: accept t152 while the second look was [superseded], prevented=False. ep5: early_alias at t127 (graded correct, 19 ticks before the smoke commit) did not produce an accept, the takeover continued to smoke, prevented=False — whereas early_correct (ep3 t92, ep9 t178, ep10 t12, ep8 t163) and alias_in_window (ep4 t115) did. Diagnosis: harness — (a) release does not cancel the in-flight block command and commit is then judged on its completion; (b) the cook's status line apparently does not treat early_alias as CORRECT, or the cook ignored it.
  5. A-scheduling — block commands complete after release. Medium (high where it flips prevention), 3 episodes / 4 cases. ep4 t123 go to the plates (8 ticks after release, delayed reaching a smoking fryer), ep8 t25 chop, ep8 t170 go to the meat source, ep10 t19 go to the cutting board. Diagnosis: harness — "everything still queued is dropped" is implemented for queued commands but not for the command already executing.
  6. F-grading — inconsistent grading of true post-release / uninjected observations; late flags not recorded. Medium–high, 5 episodes. Right mistake described while the plate was still wrong, shortly after release: ep3 t45 (6 ticks) false_alarm, ep6 t117 (7 ticks) false_alarm, ep9 t203 (7 ticks) true_observation. True uninjected wrong-order plate: ep10 t127 false_alarm. Late flags missing from the sub-case flags: list because it is archived at release: ep2 t59, ep5 t12. The transcript does not print the flag name, so V3 cannot be fully verified. Diagnosis: grader — the late window and the "kitchen supports it" check seem to be keyed on the flag name matching a checker, so a correct description under a sibling name (e.g. wrong_order for an add_foreign plate) falls to false_alarm; archive-at-release drops later flags from the record.
  7. B-cook — V4 silence rule held only for short "attention" moments; narration and false "serving now" claims during holds. Medium, 7/10. Silent as intended: ep1 t49/t53, ep3 t84/t88, ep8 t8–t20, ep10 t8. Non-silent: ep1 t58 (argued: "Too late, I already grabbed the cheese"), ep4 t213–t227, ep5 t138–t153, ep6 t346/t386, ep7 t236–t290, ep8 t283–t331 (incl. "Thanks for the help, that went pretty smoothly!" after the assistant's false "Served!"), ep9 t159–t171 and t448–t496. Diagnosis: cook prompt — the "say nothing" instruction is not binding for move=answer during long holds; the cook also has no body-state check before claiming it is walking to the pass.
  8. Case design — cooks self-repair unflagged ruinous plates immediately after release. Medium, 4 episodes. ep3 t40 (extra bun), ep5 t10 (raw meat), ep6 t70 and t112 (raw tomato ×2), ep9 t196 (raw fish). The assistant's miss had no outcome cost in any of them. Diagnosis: harness/prompt — after release the cook re-plans from its full state map, which shows the wrong plate; ruinous errors are only ruinous when the cook does not look (ep1, whose omit block ran through serve).
  9. A-scheduling / case design — prep-phase anchors unreachable under tight pace. Medium, 5 episodes, 7 errors lost. plate=[] anchors requiring all components in processing before the first plating: ep4 add_foreign, ep6 wrong_order, ep7 search + omit_subgoal, ep10 leave_unattended (+add_foreign via the meat substitution); ep1 search via the omit. Tight pace (and the assistant's own correct smoke advice) forces early plating. 21 of 30 planned errors fired. Diagnosis: case design — the designer's clean trajectory preps everything first; live cooks plate as soon as something is done.
  10. A-scheduling — voice asks mistimed. Medium, 5 episodes. Proactive "shout when X is done" asked after X was already COOKED (ep9 t61, +14 ticks; ep10 t94, +14 ticks). Questions anchored on the final plating step asked on the serve tick, never answered (ep5 t196, t204). q_ready_in never asked while its item cooked (ep8 t110–t114; ep10 four windows). Ill-posed template questions ("Did I chop the meat/fish…" for cooked-only items: ep3 t32, ep4 t74). Diagnosis: harness anchors (no "still cooking" / "time left before serve" guard) and question templates (prep verb hard-coded as "chop").
  11. D-assistant — proactive requests never fulfilled; QA answers non-responsive. Medium, 7 + 5 episodes. Every proactive request records answered tNone (ep3, ep4, ep6, ep7, ep8, ep9, ep10); in ep6 it announced "Rice is done" 5 ticks after turn-on; in ep8 it promised "I'll shout" four times while the steak smoked. QA non-answers or wrong answers: ep1 t25 (board count), ep2 t146, ep7 t224 (hallucinated pot) and t242, ep9 t196 and t456. Diagnosis: model (and possibly the capture: no episode records any proactive answer, so check that the harness captures one at all).
  12. F-grading — END labels and eval scores. Medium, 10/10. failed_at=injection_skipped in episodes with no skipped injection (ep2, ep5, ep9) and for a wrong_serve caused by a non-injected mistake (ep10); eval: blank in 8 of 10 transcripts (only ep1 0.4674 and ep3 0.7759 carry a score).
  13. C-naturalness (assistant) — error-taxonomy labels spoken aloud. Medium, 2 episodes. "this is a leave_unattended situation" (ep6 t80), "Leave-unattended alert" (ep7 t111, t170). Diagnosis: assistant prompt exposes flag names without a "never say the label" rule.
  14. D-assistant — repetition. Medium, 6 episodes. Side-quest warnings ×4–6 (ep1, ep5, ep7), "bun first, then meat, then cheese" ×14 (ep3), "trash the wrong-order plate" ×30 (ep10), "If the window rejects it…" ×5 (ep7).
  15. E-engine — unexecutable scoop hangs instead of rejecting. Medium, 1 episode. ep9 t276–t287 and t289–t313 (11 and 24 ticks, ended [superseded]), while other illegal commands are illegal-skipped with a reason.
  16. A-scheduling — skip-reason wording. Low, 3 episodes. stage_passed for divergent plates (ep1 t57, ep10 t110/t392); anchor_never_reached (waiting: cannot compose on the live kitchen) where the anchor never held (ep7); (waiting: assemble(fish) already done) for what is a stage pass (ep4).
  17. A-scheduling — forget_next_step holds of 62 ticks. Low, 4 episodes (ep6, ep7, ep8, ep9); legal, but with a finished plate in hand it adds ~60 idle ticks to every episode that reaches it.

What this assistant does well

  • Catches detours and wrong ingredients early in words. Lettuce side quest named within 4–8 ticks (ep1 t25, ep7 t24); cheese mis_prep at t8 (ep8), onion at t8 (ep10).
  • Several clean preventions. early_correct/valid flags followed by an accept before commit: ep3 t92 (wrong_order), ep8 t163 (add_foreign), ep9 t178 (leave_unattended), ep10 t12 (mis_prep); every leave_unattended that fired was eventually flagged correctly (ep4, ep5, ep7, ep9).
  • Real smoke rescues outside injections. ep4 t57 ("once the fish is ready, don't leave it sitting on the hot fryer … removing the food is how you prevent smoking"), ep6 t80, ep7 t111, ep8 t108, ep5 t159 — each followed by the cook scooping before fire. No episode burned.
  • Handles false-premise questions gracefully ("No chopping needed for the meat at all", ep3 t36; "you don't chop fish for this; you cook it as-is", ep4 t78) and gives correct memory answers when it does answer (ep6 t338/t346, ep8 t283, ep4 t201).
  • Order-aware rescue advice when it has the rules right (ep8 t114: put the tortilla on first, then scoop the smoking rice).
  • Admits mistakes when corrected (ep7 t229 "You're right—my mistake"; ep9 t305 and ep10 t244 retracting the plate-transfer assumption).
  • Latency mostly low (2–10 ticks); the single 67.6 s call was capped at 30 ticks as designed (ep4 t16→t46).

Harness behaviours that held: V1 cap (ep4); V2 prevention recorded correctly in ep3 (by the letter), ep4, ep5, ep7, ep8 (add_foreign), ep9, ep10; V3 late grading in ep2 t59 and ep5 t12; V4 silence at ep1 t49/t53, ep3 t84/t88, ep8 t8–t20, ep10 t8; V5 grounding of every marked planning-skill step by the baseline cooks (ep1, ep5, ep7, ep8 — no pasted marked step, no "I need grounding" refusal for a skill step); V6 novice self-direction after three unanswered asks (ep2, ep9 t364, ep10 t250) and acting on done food without waiting (ep9, ep10). No body error was injected while a station smoked; asks were always ≥8 ticks apart; no mistake committed on its injection tick; burn timing (COOKED → +25 smoke → +60 fire) was consistent everywhere.
